Pursuant to A.R.S. §38-431.02(H), the public body will have physical access to the meeting place one (1) hour prior to the start of
the meeting. The Board may vote to meet in a closed executive session to discuss certain matters and for legal advice on any item
as indicated on the following agenda, pursuant to A.R.S. § 38-431.03(A)(3). Items on the agenda may be considered out of order at
the Chairperson's discretion. A copy of the agenda background material provided to the Board, except items to be considered in the
executive session, is available for public inspection at the Navajo County website, www.navajocountyaz.gov 
WATCH THE MEETING LIVE AT: www.navajocountyaz.gov/660
All public comments will need to be made in person or in writing. Written comments will be received by the Clerk of the Board's
office twenty-four (24) hours prior to the Board meeting, at melissa.buckley@navajocountyaz.gov. Persons with disabilities who
need accommodation to attend or participate in the meeting may contact the Clerk of the Board’s office at least forty-eight (48)
hours prior to the meeting, so accommodation can be arranged.

SHERIFF'S OFFICE:
 
a. Consideration and adoption of a proclamation declaring the week of May
11th - 17th, 2025, as National Police Week in Navajo County, Arizona :
Sheriff David Clouse thanked Navajo County for annually adopting
proclamations recognizing and supporting law enforcement. He indicated
that the support extends not only to the Navajo County Sheriff's deputies
but to all law enforcement officers serving in the county. He acknowledged
their continuous dedication, working 24/7. He noted that National
Correctional Officers Week is observed first, followed by Police Week later
in the month.  He requested that the Board to adopt May 11–17 as
National Police Week in Navajo County, aligning with the national
observance proclaimed by President Kennedy in 1962. He stated that May
15th is recognized as National Peace Officer Memorial Day, honoring
officers who died in the line of duty, noting that a memorial ceremony will
be held that evening in Show Low at 6:00 p.m. He spoke about honoring
both fallen and active law enforcement officers and thanked the Sheriff's
Office staff for their efforts in organizing and supporting Police Week.  He
read the proposed proclamation. 
Vice-Chair Whitesinger thanked Sheriff Clouse and expressed gratitude
to all who serve the community, highlighting the importance of taking time
to appreciate their dedication and the sacrifices they make.
Vice-Chair Whitesinger made a motion to adopt a proclamation
declaring the week of May 11th - 17th, 2025, as National Police Week in
Navajo County, Arizona, motion seconded by Supervisor Benally.
Supervisor Benally thanked the Sheriff, deputies, staff, and police officers
across Navajo County for their service. She spoke about attending a past
memorial event in Show Low honoring fallen officers and express
appreciation, well wishes, and hopes for everyone's safety and protection.
 
Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Daryl Seymore being absent.
 
  
b. Consideration and adoption of a proclamation declaring the week of May   
                                                                    2 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 9 -->
b. Consideration and adoption of a proclamation declaring the week of May
4th - 10th, 2025, as National Correctional Officers Week in Navajo County,
Arizona, in honor of the men and women whose diligence and
professionalism keep our county and citizens safe :
Sheriff David Clouse requested that the Board designate May 4–10 as
National Correctional Officers Week in Navajo County, recognizing the
vital role correctional officers play in the criminal justice system. He spoke
of the demanding and often thankless nature of the job and praised the
detention staff for maintaining one of Arizona’s cleanest and safest jail
systems. He gave special recognition to Commander Garcia and his team
for upholding national accreditation standards.  He read portions of the
proposed proclamation.
 
Chairman Whiting thanked Sheriff Clouse for recognizing and expressing
appreciation for his team and the valuable work they do.
Supervisor Benally made a motion to adopt a proclamation declaring
the week of May 4th - 10th, 2025, as National Correctional Officers Week
in Navajo County, Arizona, in honor of the men and women whose
diligence and professionalism keep our county and citizens safe, motion
seconded by Vice-Chair Whitesinger.
 
Supervisor Benally thanked the correctional officers, acknowledging the
challenges of their job.  She shared a personal connection, noting that
family members serve in the role, and express gratitude to all correctional
officers and sheriff's department staff for their dedication to protecting the
community.
 
Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Daryl Seymore being absent.
 
  
PUBLIC WORKS:
 
a. Consideration of the award of Contract Number B25-02-005 for Starlight
Pavement Reconstruction to PAP, LLC:
Madhav Mundle presented a contract for a pavement reconstruction
project in the Starlight Ridge Estates Subdivision, located in District Five. 
He indicated that the project involves rebuilding sections of several roads
using new materials and asphalt. He stated that four bids were received,
and that PAP, LLC was the lowest responsive bidder. He noted that the
project is part of the county's CIP program, with a second phase planned.
He stated that staff recommends awarding the contract to PAP, LLC.
Vice-Chair Whitesinger expressed appreciation for the efforts to address
long-standing concerns about road conditions in the Starlight community.
She acknowledged the work done to understand the community’s needs
and find sustainable solutions for road recovery and long-term
maintenance.
Vice-Chair Whitesinger made a motion to approve the award of
Contract Number B25-02-005 for Starlight Pavement Reconstruction to
  
                                                                    3 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 10 -->
PAP, LLC, motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.
Chairman Whiting confirmed that the contract must go to the lowest
qualified bidder, in accordance with the county’s procurement policy. He
expressed a desire for a local preference in bidding but acknowledge that
current laws do not allow for such a preference.
 
Motion passed 4-0, with Supervisor Daryl Seymore being absent.
 
b. Consideration of Attachment D to The Navajo Nation Intergovernmental
Agreement for assistance with roadway maintenance materials for the
N8065 Road Maintenance Project:
Nicole Reske stated that the Forest Lake Chapter, in coordination with the
Navajo Division of Transportation, has requested assistance for the N8065
road maintenance project. She indicated that the unpaved road serves
homes, businesses, school buses, residents, and first responders. She
stated that the project involves placing aggregate base gravel over 0.8
miles, with an estimated cost of $99,952, noting that there is sufficient
capacity in the HURF budget. She indicated that staff recommend
approval and that the project aligns with the county's transportation
partnership process.
 
Supervisor Benally expressed gratitude to Nicole and others for their
help with the road maintenance project. She spoke of the challenges of
muddy roads, especially after rain, and emphasize how graveling stabilizes
the road, making it safer for school buses and residents.  
Supervisor Benally made a motion to approve Attachment D to The
Navajo Nation Intergovernmental Agreement for assistance with
roadway maintenance materials for the N8065 Road Maintenance Project,
motion seconded by Supervisor Peshlakai.  Motion passed 4-0, with
Supervisor Daryl Seymore being absent.
 
  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE: Report from County Manager, County Attorney
and Board members :
Supervisor Peshlakai reported on the ongoing community cleanups for Earth
Day and visits to various chapters, noting that he has been providing quarterly
reports to chapters.  He spoke about the coordination with the Tribal Services
team on transportation projects, and the upcoming appointment of new chapter
officials. He further spoke about the planning of an intergenerational conference
for youth and elders, the dedication of a new veterans' building in White Cone, and
the installation of signs for the Korean War Veterans Memorial Highway, with
support from the Public Works team.
Supervisor Benally reported on attending CSA and a meeting with a House
Representative regarding road improvements in District One, particularly dirt
roads. She spoke about attending the Forest Lake Senior Center's renovation
event, where local communities were involved. She reported on participating in
Earth Day cleanups in different areas of her district.  She indicated that she met
with BIA about road issues, noting that they had a motor grader breakdown. She
wished everyone well for upcoming graduations and major holidays.
Vice-Chair Whitesinger expressed gratitude for the successful Wildland Fire
                                                                    4 of 5 
DRAFT

<!-- page 11 -->
Expo in March, highlighting over 200 attendees and support from partners like
Lowe's and Home Depot, who provided buckets for emergency kits. She spoke
about attending the Tribal Council meeting with different county officials, to
strengthen partnerships and communications, particularly regarding public health
services and rural Arizona's challenges. She reported on participating in several
community events, including Earth Day cleanup efforts and the Darrin Reed
Dinner, which supports scholarships for local high school students.  She shared
updates on a bus turnaround project in Cibecue, the upcoming town halls in
Whiteriver, Pinetop, and Cibecue, and her involvement with the Native Women’s
Scholarship Fund, which raises funds for local Native American women’s
education. She spoke about several cultural events. supporting Native Women’s
Scholarships, that are scheduled for May, including art gallery walks and
presentations on Apache culture.
 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC:
Michellsey Benally, a member of the Navajo Nation, provided an update on recent
legislative developments regarding rural groundwater management that could
impact the region. She discussed Senate Bill 1520, which proposes the creation of
Basin Management Areas (BMAs) for certain rural regions but excludes areas like
Navajo County, which lacks similar groundwater management options. She also
highlighted Senate Bill 1425 and House Bill 2714, which propose creating Rural
Groundwater Management Areas (RGMAs) with local councils but were not heard
in committee. She indicated that while these efforts focus on southern and
western Arizona, there has been no representation from northern Arizona, leaving
the region without management tools for potential groundwater challenges. She
encouraged the Board to stay engaged and mentioned an upcoming Rural
Groundwater Resilience Workshop on June 18, 2025, that will focus on the Little
Colorado River Plateau Basin and urged the Board to advocate for inclusive
groundwater management tools for northern Arizona.
 
ADJOURN: 9:46 a.m. meeting adjourned.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. c.        
Meeting Date: 05/13/2025  
Title: Call of Election for Silver Creek Flood Protection Dist.
Submitted By: Rayleen Richards, Elections Director
Department: Elections
Motion before the Board:
Approval to conduct an election for the Silver Creek Flood Protection District on October 1, 2025, to
elect 3 positions with 1 position for a 4-year term and 2 positions for a 2-year term 
Background:
To fill 2 position(s) on the District Board beginning OCTOBER 2025 and ending OCTOBER 2027.1.
To fill 1 position on the District Board beginning OCTOBER 2025 and ending OCTOBER 2029.2.
Due to Agenda Item 3906 dated 02/27/2024.
The Silver Creek Flood Control District did not hold an election in October 2023, leaving two vacant
seats; therefore, the Board of Supervisors needs to appoint qualified candidates to the position. The Silver
Creek Flood Control District as well as Navajo County has requested that interested members submit a
letter of interest to the Navajo County Board of Supervisors. We have received three letters of interest.
48-2681. Election of directors; qualification; vacancies A. In each district organized under this chapter an
election shall be held on the first Wednesday in October of the calendar year next succeeding that in
which the organization of the district is completed, and on the first Wednesday in October each second
year thereafter, at which one or more directors for the district as provided in section 48-2607 shall be
elected to fill the vacancy or vacancies occurring on the board by expiration of the term of office of one
or more members of the board as provided in section 48-2615. The persons receiving the highest number
of votes for the offices to be filled at the election shall be declared elected thereto. B. Within ten days
after receiving their respective certificates of election, each of the persons elected shall qualify as a
director by taking and subscribing the official oath and filing a bond as provided by this section. Each
director shall execute an official bond in the amount of one thousand dollars, which shall be approved by
the judge of the superior court in the county where the organization of the district was affected, and
recorded in the office of the county recorder of such county. The official bond together with the official
oath shall be filed with the secretary of the board of directors. Official bonds shall be in the form
prescribed by law for official bonds of county officers. C. If a vacancy occurs on the board of directors, it
shall be filled by appointment by the board of supervisors of the county in which the district is organized.
A director so appointed shall qualify within ten days after receiving notice of his appointment in the same
manner as provided for directors elected to the office, and shall hold office only until the next regular
election for the district and until his successor is elected and qualified.

Board of Supervisors Regular 1. e.        
Meeting Date: 05/13/2025  
Title: IGA Between Superior Courts of Greenlee and Navajo Counties
Submitted For: Jason Cash, Chief Probation Officer 
Submitted By: Laura Mudge, Chief Financial Officer
Department: Probation
Motion before the Board:
Intergovernmental Agreement between Superior Courts of Greenlee and Navajo Counties for Juvenile
Detention Services
 
Background:
The purpose of this IGA is for the provision of juvenile detention (“Services”) for up to 10 of
Sender’s juvenile detainees (Sender juvenile(s)) transferred by Sender to Host Facility. Sender
acknowledges and agrees that Host’s policies and practices may not necessarily coincide with those of
Sender and that Host and all inmates therein are managed and administered according to Host’s policies
and practices and will remain so for the duration of this IGA. Juveniles are those individuals under the
age of eighteen years. Sender acknowledges and agrees that Host will not accept juveniles: who are
charged with Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 2 of 11 nothing more than incorrigible offense(s);
who are under the age of eight years; or detainees that cannot be detained with other juveniles in a
juvenile facility pursuant to applicable law.
WHEREAS, the Parties are authorized and responsible to provide for the detention of certain juveniles
alleged to be delinquent or children who are incorrigible pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 8-305 and 8-301 et seq.
and the Parties desire to utilize available resources in and efficient and mutually beneficial
manner according to applicable law; and
WHEREAS, Navajo County maintains a juvenile detention center (Facility) where such juveniles may be
detained—which Facility is separate and apart from any adult detention center in which adults
are confined; and
WHEREAS, the Presiding Judge of the Juvenile Division of the Superior Court in Navajo
County supervises the Facility pursuant to A.R.S. § 8-306; and
WHEREAS, A.R.S. §§ 11-952(J) and 11-951 et seq. authorize the Parties to enter into this IGA.

Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 1 of 11 
 
 
INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT  
- BY AND BETWEEN - 
NAVAJO COUNTY,   
AND THE SUPERIOR COURTS OF THE STATE OF ARIZONA IN AND FOR NAVAJO 
COUNTY,  
AND GREENLEE COUNTY,   
AND THE SUPERIOR COURTS OF THE STATE OF ARIZONA IN AND FOR GREENLEE 
COUNTY 
- FOR - 
JUVENILE DETENTION SERVICES  
 
THIS INTERGOVERNMENTAL AGREEMENT dated this 25th day of March, 2025 (“IGA”), is made 
by and between the Sender  and the Host, as set forth herein, for the detention of juveniles under the 
supervision of Sending Party. The parties to this IGA include Navajo County, a political subdivision of the 
State of Arizona (“Host County”), and the Superior Court of Arizona in and for Navajo County, on behalf 
of the Navajo County Juvenile Detention Center ( “Host Facility”) (Host County and Host Facility may be 
collectively referred to as “Host ”) —and— Greenlee County(“Sending Authority”), and the Superior 
Court of Arizona in and for Greenlee County, on behalf of the Greenlee County Probation Department 
(“Sending Department”) (Sending County and Sending Department may be collectively referred to as 
“Sender”). The parties to this agreement may also be referred to individually as “Party” or collectively as 
“Parties” hereinafter. 
 
RECITALS 
WHEREAS, the Parties are authorized and responsible to provide for the detention of certain 
juveniles alleged to be delinquent or children who are incorrigible pursuant to A.R.S. §§ 8-305 and 8-301 
et seq. and the Parties desire to utilize available resources in and efficient and mutually beneficial manner 
according to applicable law; and  
WHEREAS, Navajo County maintains a juvenile detention center (Facility) where such juveniles 
may be detained—which Facility is separate and apart from any adult detention center in which adults are 
confined; and 
WHEREAS, the Presiding Judge of the Juvenile Division of the Superior Court in Navajo County 
supervises the Facility pursuant to A.R.S. § 8- 306; and  
WHEREAS, A.R.S. §§ 11-952(J) and 11-951 et seq. authorize the Parties to enter into this IGA.  
 
AGREEMENT 
NOW, THEREFORE, the Parties hereto, in consideration of the stipulations, covenants and 
agreements hereinafter set forth, do hereby agree as follows:  
 
1. PURPOSE AND INTENT 
The purpose of this IGA is for the provision of juvenile detention (“Services”) for up to 10 of  Sender’s 
juvenile detainees (Sender juvenile(s)) transferred by Sender to Host  Facility. Sender acknowledges 
and agrees that Host’s policies and practices may not necessarily coincide with those of Sender and that 
Host and all inmates therein are managed and administered according to Host’s policies and practices 
and will remain so for the duration of this IGA. Juveniles are those individuals under the age of eighteen 
years. Sender acknowledges and agrees that Host will not accept juveniles : who are charged with

<!-- page 44 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 2 of 11 
 
 
nothing more than incorrigible offense(s); who are under the age of eight years; or detainees that cannot 
be detained with other juveniles in a juvenile facility pursuant to applicable law .  
2. EFFECTIVE DATE AND DURATION 
A. Initial Term. Upon execution and signature below, this IGA shall become effective on the 25th 
day of March , 2025,  and shall remain in effect for one (1) year thereafter unless otherwise 
terminated or renewed as provided in this IGA.  
B. Termination. Either Party may terminate this IGA, with or without cause, by providing thirty (30) 
days’ advance written notice of termination to the other Party as set forth in Section 9.A.  E ach 
Party agrees to return any and all equipment and/or materials in its control or possession, if any, to 
the owner-Party no later than thirty (30) days after termination.  
C. Renewal. By mutual written agreement of the Parties, this IGA may be renewed for no more than 
nine (9) additional one -year terms (Subsequent Term(s)) pursuant to A.R.S. § 11- 952(J).   
3. COSTS AND FEES. The Parties agree that a per diem rate per juvenile will be charged, and are to be 
paid by Sender under Section 4.B.ii below.   
4. MUTUAL OBLIGATIONS 
A. Under this IGA Host agrees to: 
i. Provide detention services (“Services”) for Sender juveniles in Host Facility according 
to applicable policies, practices, standards and requirements.  
Services provided shall include, but are not to be limited to, housing, food, 
clothing, normal hygiene, and other routine services and care, including routine 
medical and mental health care, education, recreation, and visitation. All Services will 
be provided in compliance with applicable laws, ordinances, state and federal standards 
and practices.  
All juveniles referred to Host by Sender will be treated according to the same rules 
and regulations applied to other detainees in the custody of the Host Facility.  
ii. Send billing invoices to Sender  on a monthly basis per Section 6 below. 
iii. Manage the day-to-day supervision, operations and services for Sender juveniles. 
iv. Provide all staff, labor and services necessary for the Facility’s day -to-day use and 
operation and maintain and provide all staff, labor, materials, and routine in- house 
services and bear all routine costs associated with the Facility at no additional cost to 
Sender. 
v. Arrange for non- routine medical, mental health, and/or educational services by 
juveniles referred by Sender and detained at the Facility. These non -routine services 
include, but are not limited to, hospitalization, ambulance, psychiatric assessments, 
psych-ed evaluation, and medications.  
The Parties acknowledge and agree that non -routine services will result in 
additional expense(s) which are the sole responsibility of Sender .  
To the extent permitted by time, Host shall contact Sender to discuss any non-
routine services that arise. The Parties agree that such discussion shall not unduly delay 
any need for emergency services, including, but not limited to emergency medical 
attention. Host shall have the sole discretion and authority to determine whether a 
situation is an emergency, or becomes an emergency while awaiting Sender’s 
concurrence under Section 3.D below and Sender shall defer to the Host’s assessment

<!-- page 45 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 3 of 11 
 
 
of the situation and determination regarding the emergency / non-emergency nature of 
the situation.  
vi. To the extent permitted by time, Host shall contact Sender prior to any non- routine 
services that arise and in order to discuss and concur in writing regarding said 
service(s) with Sender.  
vii. In the event of any emergency situation(s) and/or service(s) involving Sender 
juvenile(s), Host will notify Sender  of within 24 hours of occurrence.  
viii. Provide twenty-four hours advance notice when Sender will need to retrieve a juvenile. 
ix. Observe and administer any records exchanged under this IGA pursuant to applicable 
law. 
B. Under this IGA Sender agrees to: 
i. Promptly contact Host Intake Unit at (928) 205-8395, prior to transporting a juvenile 
to the Facility for admission. Sender  will also provide Host with: 
1. Applicable Court Order and/or Referral and Affidavit to detain the juvenile ; and  
2. Any available records or information requested by Host including but not limited 
to, information regarding the juvenile's family history, behavioral issues, medical, 
mental health, psychological evaluations, school, and/or social history for 
admission and monitoring purposes. 
ii. Pay a fee of $2 25.00 per day per Sender juvenile detained.  
1. Pay an additional fee of $50.00 per day for each day a Sender juvenile is 
detained for more than 90 days.  
For the purposes of this subsection, ‘day’ means the juvenile  is incarcerated in the 
Facility for more than two (2) hours within a calendar day. The standard daily fee may 
not be increased without the written agreement of the Parties.  
iii. Pay any and all  Additional Expense(s) incurred under or arising from this IGA and 
reimburse Host for any and all Additional Expense(s) incurred by Host under this IGA. 
iv. Pay each invoice received no later than thirty (30) days after receiving the invoice per 
Section 6. 
v. Provide transportation to and from the Facility: when Sender juveniles are booked into 
and released from the Facility; when appearances are required at court hearings; and 
when medical, dental, or other appointments for any such juvenile are scheduled 
within/outside the Holbrook area. When available, Host staff may assist on transports 
within the Holbrook area. 
vi. Sender will not deliver to the Facility any juveniles under the influence of controlled 
substances, or experiencing serious medical or mental health concerns, including self-
harm behavior, without first obtaining a medical release from an appropriate medical 
and/or mental health professional or hospital.  
vii. Provide any records necessitated by the purposes of this IGA. Such records may 
include, but are not necessarily limited to, juvenile penalogical and discipline records, 
criminal background and history records, medical records, and mental health records. 
viii. Remain responsible for the transportation of juveniles to and from the Host facility. 
Notwithstanding this, in the event of a medical emergency or other appropriate 
circumstance Host may transport a Sender juvenile from the Host Facility  to the

<!-- page 46 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 4 of 11 
 
 
appropriate destination and Sender agrees that such instances may result in Additional 
Expense(s). 
ix. Ensure that no juvenile shall be held at Facility for more than twenty-four (24) hours 
unless a petition alleging delinquent conduct or a criminal complaint has been filed 
and a copy provided to the Host Intake unit. No juvenile  shall be held longer than 
twenty four hours after the filing of a petition unless so ordered by the court after a 
hearing.  
If a hearing is not held within twenty- four hours after the filing of the petition, 
Sender shall ensure that the juvenile shall be released from Facility to a parent, 
guardian, custodian or other responsible person. {NOTE: Juvenile Court requires 24 
hours petition filed when detained and a hearing 24 hours after petition is filed.}   If 
no parent, guardian, custodian or other responsible person can be located, Facility shall 
release the juvenile to the Department of Child Safety and Host shall notify Sending 
County Juvenile Court of the release.  
x. Observe and administer any records exchanged under this IGA pursuant to applicable 
law. 
xi. Upon notice from Host, promptly retrieve each Sender juvenile from the Host Facility 
not later than 24 hours after such notice from Host.   
C. Facility Capacity. The Parties acknowledge and agree that housing Sender juveniles will not be 
available if/when the population of the portion(s) of the Facility in operation at the time of referral 
is at capacity. If the Facility is at or near capacity, Host will work with Sender to determine which 
juveniles under the supervision of Sender should be released in the event the need arises for 
additional detention of juveniles at the Facility.  
D. Written Concurrence for Non-Routine Services. In the event of non-routine services the Parties 
agree to discuss said services and create a written concurrence regarding the services in advance. 
The Parties acknowledge that any emergency circumstances that arise may, in turn, make it 
unreasonable or unfeasible to discuss and/or create a written concurrence in advance.  
E. Additional Expenses . Additional Expense(s) include any and all costs associated with: 
transportation not expressly provided for in this IGA that are attributable to Sender juveniles; non-
routine services and/or materials required by Sender juveniles; and Sender juvenile medical and/or 
mental-health care and treatment not covered by the daily inmate fee referenced above. M edical 
and/or mental-health care and treatment not covered by the daily inmate fee include: prescription 
medication(s), hospital visits or  any other medical/mental -health services requiring outside 
facilities, services or providers, and/or catastrophic medical or mental -health events.  
F. Remote Appearance Technology . To the maximum extent possible, and according to the 
mandates of the Court, the Parties intend to utilize available technology for the remote 
appearance/attendance of juveniles in hearings, proceedings, meetings, appointments, and 
evaluations when appropriate and available. Such means/methods may include audio/video 
conferencing technology and/or telephonic communication. The purpose of doing so is to 
maximum the health and safety of all facility occupants and to maxim ize the efficient use of 
available time, staff and resources, subject to any requisite court approval or, when applicable, the 
professional discretion of involved physician(s), other psychiatric or medical professional(s), or 
legal counsel.

<!-- page 47 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 5 of 11 
 
 
G. Juvenile Legal Counsel. Host is not responsible for any legal representation required or requested 
by Sender juveniles. The provision of any such legal counsel and/or any arrangements therefore 
remain the responsibility of Sender .  
H. Sender Release. Sender shall notify Host Intake Unit at (928) 205-8395 when a Sender juvenile 
is to be released from the Facility to the custody of Sender. Any such release made at the request 
of Sender shall be at the sole discretion of the Sender and shall, with prior verbal notice confirmed 
by e-mail, be performed promptly and without undue delay by Host, and shall not require an order 
of a court. The Facility shall only release the detained juvenile upon notice from a specifically 
identified person the Sender has advised, in writing, is authorized to request release of a detained 
juvenile. The Facility shall only release the detained juvenile to a specifically identified person that 
Sender has advised, in writing, is authorized to take custody of the juvenile.  
I. Facility Acceptance or Rejection of Juvenile. Host retains final and absolute right to refuse 
acceptance or request removal of any Sender juvenile exhibiting violent or disruptive behavior, or 
of any juvenile found to have a medical condition that requires medical care beyond the scope of 
the Facility health provider. In the case of a juvenile already in Facility custody, Host shall notify 
Sender and request such removals, and shall allow Sender reasonable time to make alternative 
arrangements for the juvenile.   
J. Open Communications. Host and Sender shall maintain open communications between each 
Party’s designated point of contact [“POC”] (listed in Section 10.A) to ensure the agreed upon 
facilities and services are provided and maintained throughout the term of this IGA. Parties shall 
maintain open communication regarding needs arising out of the IGA.  
K. PREA. The Prison Rape Elimination Act (PREA) 34 U.S.C. §§ 30301—30309 and 28 C.F.R. §§ 
115.11—115.501 was established to address the elimination and prevention of sexual assault and 
sexual harassment within correctional systems and detention facilities. The Parties  will comply 
with all standards. The Navajo County Sheriff’s  Department will be assigned to investigate all 
incidents of sexual assault allegedly occurring within the Facility. The Parties agree to disclose any 
knowledge of sexual abuse or sexual harassment that a Sender  juvenile may have encountered, 
whether as the perpetrator or the victim.  
L. Emergency Evacuation. In the event of any emergency requiring evacuation of the Facility, Host 
shall evacuate the juvenile in the same manner and with the same safeguards as other juveniles in 
the Facility. Host shall verbally notify Sender and confirm by e-mail or fax, within four (4) hours 
of such evacuation. 
M. Force Majeure. The Parties are not liable to each other if an occurrence of force majeure prevents 
its performance under the contract. If either party is delayed at any time in the progress of its 
performance under the contract by an occurrence of force majeure, the delayed party shall notify 
the other no later than the following working day after the occurrence, or as soon as it could 
reasonably have been expected to recognize that the occurrence had effect in cases where the effects 
were not readily apparent. In any event, the notice must make specific reference to this paragraph 
specifying the causes of the delay in the notice and, if the effects of the occurrence are on-going, 
provide an initial notification and thereafter the delayed party shall provide regular updates until 
such time as the effects are fully known. To the extent it is able, the delayed party shall cause the 
delay to cease promptly and notify the other party when it has done so.  
N. Public Health Crises. In the event of a public health crisis or the like (i.e. COVID 19 pandemic), 
Sender acknowledges and agrees that Facility is authorized to take all reasonable measures, as 
determined in the sole discretion of Facility, to protect the health and welfare of Facility occupants.

<!-- page 48 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 6 of 11 
 
 
Such measures may include, but are not limited to, stopping all non-essential ingress and/or egress 
to and from the Facility which may result in the discontinuation of programs and/or practices that 
pose a high risk of introducing and/or transmitting infec tion to or between Facility occupants. 
Additionally, Facility reserves the right to deny entry of a juvenile referred to Facility where, as 
determined in the sole discretion of Facility, the public interest in detention does not outweigh the 
risk of introducing infection into the Facility. Further, at the request of Host, Sender may be 
required to arrange for an initial quarantine detention of any such juvenile before the juvenile may 
be later transferred into Facility.  
5. SUPERVISION 
Each Party shall have sole supervisory authority over that party’s personnel, operations, services and 
materials. Each Party agrees that it will be solely responsible for and will assume sole liability for its 
officer’s acts or omissions of any kind, while performing any service or activity under this IGA.  E ach 
Party shall remain responsible for its employees' salaries and employee-related benefits, discipline and 
similar matters and shall be solely responsible for its employee's civil wrongs, and each employee shall 
be deemed to be performing r egular duties for the primary employer Party while engaged in services 
and activities under this IGA. For the purposes of Workers' Compensation, the Party employing such 
employee shall be solely liable for the payment of Worker's Compensation benefits payable as the result 
of the employee's participation in services and activities under this IGA.  
6. EQUIPMENT AND MATERIALS 
The Parties agree to avoid using the other Party’s materials and/or equipment for purposes not directly 
associated with the purpose and intent of this IGA without the prior express written consent from the 
Party to whom the equipment and/or materials belong. However, this provision shall not be construed 
to prohibit any use of materials or equipment of another Party that is merely nominal and incidental, or 
on an emergency basis. 
7. BILLING/INVOICING 
Billing, invoicing and payment between the Parties arising out of this IGA shall be administered as 
follows: 
A. Host shall bill Sender on a monthly basis and send an itemized invoice promptly at the end of each 
month. 
B. Sender shall send payment to Host within thirty (30)  days of receiving the invoice.  
C. All billing and payment correspondence shall be sent to the Party contacts provided in Section 
10.A. 
8. INSURANCE 
Each Party acknowledges and affirms that it has appropriate and adequate insurance coverage for its 
official operations, duties and activities, and that it will maintain such coverage for the duration of this 
IGA.  
9. INDEMNIFICATION AND COOPERATIVE DEFENSE 
A. To the maximum extent permitted by law, the Parties agree to defend, indemnify and hold one 
harmless their elected officials, agents and employees, from and against any and all claims, actions, 
suits or proceedings of any kind brought against said parties because of any injury or damage 
received or sustained by person, persons, or property arising out of or resulting from the services

<!-- page 49 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 7 of 11 
 
 
performed under this Agreement.  The Host shall be indemnified against all expenses judgements, 
penalties, fines and amounts paid in settlement actually and reasonably incurred by them, or on 
their behalf, in connection with any such proceeding or any claim, issue or matter therein, arising 
from a juvenile, if the Host acted in good faith and in a manner the Host reasonably believed to be 
in or not apposed to the best interests of the Sender or the Sender juvenile.  
B. In the event that a claim, arising from or relating to the activities provided in this IGA, is made 
against any Party for acts or omissions of any of its employees or officers, it is the intent of the 
Parties to cooperate fully in the defense of said claim  or claims and to cause their insurers to do 
likewise, to the extent practicable. 
C. The obligations under this section shall survive the termination of this IGA.  
10. GENERAL 
A. NOTICES: Except as otherwise provided in this IGA, all notices to the other Party required under 
this IGA shall be in writing and sent to the following personnel:  
 
If to Sender:               
Name   
Title 
  
  
  Agency/Governmental Entity Name  
 
  ______________________________________________ 
  Address 
 
If to Host:   Director  
  Navajo County Probation Department  
  P.O. Box 668 
     Holbrook, Arizona 86025  
 
Notices under this Section shall be deemed completed and effective on the date delivered, if given 
by facsimile, personal delivery, email or overnight express delivery service, or four days after the 
date of deposit in the mail if sent through the United States Mail 
B. MODIFICATION: This IGA shall not be modified or extended except by a mutually signed 
written agreement. 
C. RELATIONSHIP OF THE PARTIES:  Each Party shall act in its individual capacity and not as 
an agent, employee, partner, joint venturer, associate, or any other representative capacity of the 
other party.  Each Party shall be solely and entirely responsible for its acts or acts of its agents and 
employees during the performance of this IGA.   This IGA shall not be construed to imply authority 
to perform any tasks, or accept any responsibility, not expressly set forth herein.  This IGA shall 
be strictly construed against the creation  of  a duty  or  responsibility  unless  the  intention  to  do  
so  is  clearly and unambiguously  set forth herein.  Nothing contained in this IGA confers any right 
to any person or entity not a party to this IGA.  
D. WAIVER OF TERMS AND CONDITIONS: The failure of either Party to insist in any one or 
more instances on performance of any of the terms or conditions of this IGA or to exercise any 
right or privilege contained herein shall not be considered as thereafter waiving such terms,

<!-- page 50 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 8 of 11 
 
 
conditions, rights or privileges, and they shall remain in full force and effect.  
E. GOVERNING LAW AND VENUE: The terms and conditions of this IGA shall be governed by 
and interpreted in accordance with the laws of the State of Arizona.  
F. INUREMENT, NONASSIGNMENT, SUBCONTRACTING: All of the terms, covenants and 
conditions of this IGA shall be binding upon, and shall inure to the benefit of, each Party and the 
successors and assigns of each Party. The Parties shall not assign nor sub- contract their rights, 
duties, or obligations under this IGA without the prior written consent of the other Parties  
G. ENTIRE AGREEMENT: This IGA represents the entire agreement between  the Parties and 
supersedes all prior negotiations, representations or agreements, either expressed or implied, 
written or oral. It is mutually understood and agreed that no alteration or variation of the terms and 
conditions of this IGA shall be valid unle ss made in writing and signed by the Parties.  
H. SEVERABILITY: If any part, term or provision of this IGA shall be held illegal, unenforceable 
or in conflict with any law, the validity of the remaining portions and provisions hereof shall not 
be affected. 
I. CONFLICTS OF INTEREST: To the extent applicable, the provisions of A.R.S. § 38- 511 
relating to cancellation of contracts due to conflicts of interest shall apply to this IGA.  
J. OTHER DUTIES IMPOSED BY LAW: Nothing in this IGA shall be construed as relieving the 
involved public agencies of any obligation or responsibility imposed on it by law.  
K. COMPLIANCE WITH CIVIL RIGHTS: To the extent applicable, the Parties agree to comply 
with A.R.S. Title 41, Chapter 9 (Civil Rights), Arizona Executive Order  2009-09 and any other 
federal or state laws relating to equal opportunity and non-discrimination, including the Americans 
with Disabilities Act. 
L. E-VERIFY, RECORDS AND AUDITS:  To the extent applicable: Under A.R.S. § 41-4401, the 
Parties and their respective subcontractors warrant compliance with all federal immigration laws 
and regulations that relate to their employees and compliance with the E-verify requirements under 
A.R.S. § 23-214(A).   The Parties' or a subcontractor's breach of the above -mentioned warranty 
shall be deemed a material breach of the IGA and may result in the termination of the IGA by either 
party under the terms of this IGA.  The Parties each retain the legal right to randomly inspect the 
papers and records of each other Party and each other Party' subcontractors who work under this 
IGA to ensure that the other party and its subcontractors are complying with the above-mentioned 
warranty. The Parties warra nt to keep their respective papers and records open for random 
inspection during normal business hours by each other Party. The Parties and their respective 
subcontractors shall cooperate with each other Party's random inspections including granting the 
inspecting Party entry rights onto their respective properties to perform the random inspections and 
waiving their respective rights to keep such papers and records confidential. The Parties agree to 
comply with the records retention requirements of A.R.S. § 35- 214 
M. INTERPARTY DISPUTE RESOLUTION: If a dispute between the Parties arises out of or relates 
to this IGA, and if the dispute cannot be settled through negotiation within sixty (60) days, the Parties 
agree to resolve all disputes arising out the or relating to this Agreement through arbitration, after 
exhausting applicable administrative review, to the extent required by A.R.S. § 12-1518 except as 
may be required by other applicable statutes..  
N. AVAILABILITY OF FUNDS: In accordance with A.R.S. § 35-154, every payment obligation of 
each party under this Agreement is conditioned upon the availability of funds appropriated or 
allocated for the payment of such obligation.  If funds are not allocated and available for the

<!-- page 51 -->
Greenlee Juvenile Detention IGA - Page 9 of 11 
 
 
continuance of this Agreement, this agreement may be terminated by either party at the end of the 
period for which funds are available.  No liability shall accrue to either party in the event this 
provision is exercised, and the parties shall not be obligated or liable for any future payments or 
for any damages as a result of termination under this paragraph.  
O. WORKER’S COMPENSATION:    To the extent applicable, each Party shall comply with the 
notice of A.R.S. § 23-1022(E). For purposes of A.R.S. § 23-1022, each Party shall be considered 
the primary employer of all personnel currently or hereafter employed by that Party, irrespective 
of the operations of protocol in place, and said Party shall have the sole responsibility for the 
payment of Worker’s Compensation benefits or other fringe benefits of said employees.  
P. COMPLIANCE WITH LAWS AND POLICIES:   To the extent applicable, the Parties shall 
comply with all federal, state and local laws, rules, regulations, standards and Executive Orders, 
without limitation to those designated within this IGA.  The laws and regulations of the State of 
Arizona shall govern the rights of the Parties, the performance of this IGA and any disputes 
hereunder. Furthermore, the Parties agree to abide by each Party’s policies to the extent appropriate 
and required or permitted by law.  
Q. NO JOINT VENTURE:   It is not intended by this IGA to, and nothing contained in this IGA 
shall, be construed to, create any partnership, joint venture or employment relationship between the 
Parties or create any employer -employee relationship between the Parties’ employees . Neither 
Party shall be liable for any debts, accounts, obligations or other liabilities whatsoever of the other 
Party, including, but without limitation, the other Party's obligation to withhold Social Security and 
income taxes for itself or any of its employees.  
R. NO THIRD PARTY BENEFICIARIES:   Nothing in this IGA is intended to create duties or 
obligations to or rights in third parties not Parties to this IGA or affect the legal liability of either 
Party to the IGA by imposing any standard of care with respect to the maintenance of public 
facilities different from the standard of care imposed by law.  
S. COUNTERPARTS. This IGA may be executed in any number of counterparts, each of which 
shall be deemed a duplicate original and all of which when taken together shall constitute one and 
the same document. Counterparts are effective and binding when this IGA has been executed by 
all of the Parties 
T. HEADINGS: The section headings throughout this IGA shall not be used in the construction or 
interpretation hereof as they have no substantive effect and are for convenience only.

Master Services Agreement 
 
THIS Agreement together with Attachment A (collectively, the "Terms") is by and between Navajo 
County, AZ, (“Navajo County” or “Client”), and Incovate Solutions LLC. (“Incovate Solutions” 
or the “Provider”). Client hereby engages Provider to provide Services, as follows: 
1. Services.  Attached hereto and incorporated herein as a part of this agreement is a 
description of the general Services to be provided by Provider under this agreement 
(“Attachment A”) and an initial Statement of Work (SOW) for specific services (“Attachment 
B”).  Additional Statements of Work may be added subsequently by reference to this 
agreement.  Provider will perform the Services in accordance with the terms of this 
agreement and specific Statements of Work . Unless otherwise specified herein, all Work 
Products and Deliverables will be provided to Navajo County in a mutually agreed to format.  
2. Timeline/Term.  The agreement will become effective upon full execution. The anticipated 
Term (as such term is hereinafter defined) is as set forth in Section 3 of Attachment A, and 
may be extended by mutual agreement.  
3. Project Managers/Contact.   
• Provider Point of Contact (POC): Jeffrey Coe, Executive Cybersecurity Advisor, 
Incovate Solutions, LLC. 
• Client Representative: Ken Dewitt, IT Director, Navajo County  
4. Provider Personnel.  Provider staff as needed to support specific SOWs. 
5. Payment Terms. Navajo County will compensate Provider  for the Services performed 
hereunder in accordance with Section 8 of Attachment A  or as provided in the individual 
SOWs.   
6. Additional Terms and Assumptions. 
All terms and conditions of the Agreement apply to each SOW.  In the event of a conflict between 
the terms of the Agreement and a specific SOW, the terms of the SOW will prevail for purposes of 
that SOW only. 
 
IN WITNESS WHEREOF, the parties hereto have caused this agreement to be executed by their 
duly authorized representatives, effective as of the date of the last party to sign below.

Attachment A – General Provisions: 
 
 
1. Services: 
 
a. Performance of Services.  Navajo County hereby engages Provider to provide 
consulting services, as further detailed on the attached Scope of Work (“SOW”) 
(collectively, the "Services").  The parties hereby agree that if additional or different 
services are required of Provider that the parties will amend or enter into a separate 
SOW to this Agreement to, among other things, provide for and describe such 
additional or different services.  Such additional or different services shall at that time 
be considered a part of the "Services" herein.    
  
b. Services Warranty.  The Provider represents and warrants that any Services 
performed by the Provider will (i) comply with all applicable statutory rules and 
regulations, (ii) shall not knowingly or willfully infringe or misappropriate any patents, 
copyrights, trademarks, trade names, trade secrets or other intellectual property 
rights, and (iii) be performed (1) in a good, efficient, professional and workmanlike 
manner with at least the degrees of accuracy, quality, efficiency, completeness, 
timeliness and responsiveness as are equal to or higher than the accepted industry 
standards applicable to the performance of the same or similar Services, (2) using 
sufficient numbers of personnel who have suitable competence, ability, education, 
training and other qualifications for any assigned roles, (3) using then-current 
technology, and (4) in material compliance with the requirements of this Agreement.    
  
c. No Grant of License.  Neither this Agreement nor the performance of the Services 
shall in any way constitute a grant of any patent, copyright or other right or license of 
any kind absent a separate written agreement between the parties.    
   
2. Term.  This Agreement shall begin on the Effective Date and continue in effect through 
December 31, 2026, unless terminated as provided herein.  This Agreement may be 
renewed upon mutual written agreement of the parties; however, in no event shall this 
Agreement, including renewals, continue past five (5) years from the Effective Date.  
  
3. Termination.    
a. Termination for Convenience.  Client may terminate this Agreement for convenience, 
in whole or in part, at any time upon thirty (30) days’ written notice to Provider. Upon 
notification by Client, Provider shall immediately stop all work under this Agreement 
and shall immediately cause any of its suppliers and/or subcontractors to cease such 
work.  Provider shall be paid for the work and direct expenses performed prior to the 
notice of termination.  Notwithstanding the foregoing, in no event shall Provider be 
entitled to an amount greater than what Client would have paid absent the 
termination.  
  
b. Termination for Cause.  Client may terminate this Agreement, in whole or in part, in 
the event of (i) any default by Provider, (ii) Provider’s failure to comply with the terms 
and conditions of this Agreement, or (iii) Provider’s failure to comply with any specific 
terms and conditions contained in an applicable SOW.  In the event Client terminates 
this Agreement for cause, Provider shall be paid for the work and direct expenses 
performed prior to the notice of termination.  
  
c. Termination for Financial Insecurity.  Client may terminate this Agreement, in whole 
or in part, as of the date specified in a termination notice if Provider (i) files for 
bankruptcy, (ii) becomes or is declared insolvent, (iii) is the subject of any 
proceeding(s) (not dismissed within 30 days) related to its liquidation, insolvency or

<!-- page 73 -->
Master Services Agreement and SOW #1  Navajo County 
 
Incovate Solutions LLC.   14 April 2025 
Sensitive and Confidential Information  Page 3 of 15 
the appointment of a receiver or similar officer for Provider, (iv) makes an assignment 
for the benefit of all or substantially all of its creditors, (v) takes any corporate action 
for its winding-up, dissolution, or administration, (vi) enters into an agreement for the 
extension or readjustment of substantially all of its obligations, or (vii) makes any 
material misstatement as to its financial condition.  
  
4. Compensation.  For the performance of the Services, Client shall compensate Provider 
at the agreed upon rates set forth in each SOW.  Unless otherwise agreed upon in the 
applicable SOW, all invoices shall be payable within thirty (30) days from the date of 
invoice.   Reimbursement for expenses shall only be made if reimbursement of expenses 
is provided for in this Agreement or any applicable SOW; and upon approval of an 
itemized list (with receipts) for such expenses.  
  
5. Relationship of the Parties.  The parties intend that the relationship between them 
created under this Agreement is that of an independent service provider only.  Provider is 
not to be considered an agent, partner, joint-venturer, or employee of Client, or any of 
Client’s parents, affiliates or subsidiaries for any purpose.  Client is interested only in the 
results obtained under this Agreement and, subject to compliance with the provisions 
hereof, the manner and means of performing the services are subject to Provider's sole 
control.  Provider shall be responsible for all state, federal and local taxes, including 
estimated taxes, and employment reporting for Provider or any employees or agents of 
Provider.  
 
6. Background and Objective.  
 
Navajo County is looking for guidance for its information security program to help manage 
cyber risk as it continues to grow its business.  Specifically, Client is looking for guidance 
on the security program, tools, processes, and team needed to better protect its residents 
and assets while managing risk.  Client leadership and Information Technology team will 
collaborate with Incovate Solutions through this MSA and individual Statements of Work 
for specific projects.  
 
7. Project Managers/Contact.  
 
o Provider Project Lead: Jeffrey Coe, Executive Cybersecurity Advisor, Incovate 
Solutions 
o Client Representative: Ken Dewitt, IT Director, Navajo County  
Navajo County understands that the Incovate Solutions consultants will at all times 
retain their current employment status during the course of this engagement and 
Incovate Solutions personnel are only consultants serving in a general advisory role for 
Navajo County.  
 
8. Professional Fees, Travel Expenses and Invoicing.  
 
Professional fees will be on a Time and Materials (“T&M”) basis at the rates specified in 
Table 1 below based on the seniority and experience of the staff provided, unless 
otherwise specified in a specific SOW. 
 
All reasonable travel expenses must be pre-approved by Client in writing (email is 
acceptable) and directly related to the performance of the Services required hereunder.    
Only actual incurred expenses will be billed.

9. SOW or Engagement Change Request Procedure 
The following process will be followed if a material change (scope or price change) to this 
SOW is required: 
• An Engagement Change Request (ECR) will be the vehicle for communicating 
change. The ECR must describe the change, the rationale for the change, and the 
effect the change will have on the engagement. 
• The designated Engagement Manager of the requesting party (Service Provider or 
Client) will review the proposed change and determine whether to submit the request 
to the other party. 
• Both Engagement Managers will review the proposed change and approve it for 
further investigation or reject it. Service Provider and Client will mutually agree upon 
any charges for such investigation, if any. If the investigation is authorized, the Client 
Engagement Managers will sign the ECR, which will constitute approval for the 
investigation charges. The investigation will determine the effect that the 
implementation of the ECR will have on SOW price, schedule and other terms and 
conditions of the Agreement.  
• Upon completion of the investigation, both parties will review the impact of the 
proposed change and, if mutually agreed, a Change Authorization will be executed.  
Service Provider will invoice Client for any such charges. 
• A written Change Authorization and/or ECR must be signed by both parties to 
authorize implementation of any agreed upon changes.

Attachment C – Statement of Work #1 – Fractional CISO 
(continued) 
 
1. Scope and Objectives:  
 Navajo County is looking for guidance for its information security program to help 
manage cyber risk related to Navajo County’s requirements as it continues to grow and 
support its residents.  Navajo County’s primary objective is to engage Incovate Solutions 
to provide consulting services on a part-time basis to provide some of the functions 
generally provided by a chief information security officer (CISO), a.k.a., Fractional CISO.  
  
“Virtual CISO” or “Fractional CISO” is solely a descriptive reference to the 
services being provided hereunder by Incovate Solutions, and no Incovate 
Solutions staff will have any official title or position with, or any authority to act in 
an official capacity on behalf of, Client. Incovate Solutions’ role under this SOW 
shall be that of consultant, only, providing services per the terms and conditions 
herein.  
  
Incovate Solutions will provide staffing and services  to work in a collaborative manner 
with the Client’s IT Director, information technology (IT) team, information security staff, 
and departmental teams to continue advancing the cyber security program and security 
operations, and enforce the cyber security policies and strategies formulated to manage 
risk within Client’s IT systems, processes, and practices.  The cyber security program will 
encompass various aspects of managing cyber risk across the various business functions 
as they exist today; including technical, physical, staff, and business risk, and 
recommend how to handle and mitigate cyber risk appropriately. The goal is to help 
mature and operate the cyber security program and advance key security initiatives as 
the organization matures its IT and information security programs.    
 
Navajo County leadership and Information Technology team will collaborate with Incovate 
Solutions to execute an assessment of the current program and plan improvements to 
help Navajo County’s cybersecurity program protect Navajo County’s business interests  
and critical information assets: 
• Assess the strengths and weaknesses of the cyber security program’s current 
state across people, process, and technology, using a tailored approach based 
on the National Institute of Standards and Technology (NIST) Cybersecurity 
Framework (CSF). 
• Identify prioritized gaps or practices to further improve the Navajo County’s 
current program and capabilities to an appropriate security level of maturity 
based on Navajo County’s threat environment and risk tolerance.  
• Develop a long-term strategic roadmap of prioritized recommendations to 
improve, monitor, and maintain the cybersecurity program relative to NIST C SF. 
Throughout the course of this support engagement, Client may request that Incovate 
Solutions engage in other cyber security projects and tasks, as the fulfillment of the 
responsibilities outlined above may highlight security needs and general functions in 
other areas; provided, however, that Incovate Solutions will not be obligated to perform 
any work for other projects and/or any work that is outside the scope of this Attachment 
A, unless the parties have executed a written change order or SOW Amendment se tting 
forth the mutually agreed additional services, fees, and other terms, as applicable.

<!-- page 82 -->
Master Services Agreement and SOW #1  Navajo County 
 
Incovate Solutions LLC.   14 April 2025 
Sensitive and Confidential Information  Page 12 of 15 
a) Plan Overview 
Incovate Solutions will employ an assessment tool and subject-matter expertise to assess the 
current program as a baseline understanding of the security program and capabilities. 
Incovate Solutions will review Navajo County’s risk and security program against the NIST 
CSF and Incovate Solutions’ experience to make actionable recommendations for Navajo 
County. The project will consider the current state of Navajo County’s program, it’s high-level 
strategy, and key security technologies in the portfolio of services.  The project also delivers a 
prioritized roadmap for improvement over time. This assessment will include:  
 
• Reviewing Navajo County’s current security technology program based primarily 
on the NIST CSF framework, and customized for Navajo County by Incovate 
Solutions; and 
• Incorporating the expertise of Incovate Solutions team, common cyber insurance 
qualification questions, and well-known practices for managing ransomware and 
other likely threats to Navajo County business operations; and 
• Reviewing Navajo County’s processes and activities that govern the security 
program and cyber risk management, and 
• Consideration of Navajo County’s team and skillsets that implement the security 
program and cyber risk management. 
 
b) Phases 
Incovate Solutions will execute the assessment in four phases, as described herein:  
 
i. Phase 1: Prepare – Incovate Solutions will work with Navajo County and its 
Information Security team in Phase 1 to ensure project scope, alignment, and 
timing. Incovate Solutions expects this effort to include a kickoff meeting, 
identifying critical business information assets and points-of-contact, 
validating the project plan, and finalizing the appropriate in-scope security 
controls.  
 
ii. Phase 2: Collect – Using the assessment tool, Incovate Solutions will 
customize a set of questions to be used throughout the Phase 2.  These will 
be used to focus the assessment and to document observations while 
interviewing the team identified in Phase 1.  Collection will primarily be done 
with interviews, however a document request, and quick, high-level review of 
documents provided by Navajo County will be included in Phase 2.  We will 
work to schedule interviews as quickly, and close together as possible to 
complete the work in a timely manner.   
 
iii. Phase 3: Analyze – During Phase 3 Incovate Solutions will analyze the 
collected data against the NIST CSF.  This analysis may include 
considerations of organizational structure, business strategy, the threat 
environment, risk tolerance and other factors.  Incovate Solutions will use its 
best professional judgement to assess the baseline program across the 
people, process, and technology dimensions.  Taking into consideration the 
known threats, business priorities, and current state security posture, the 
team will document a series of observations (positive and negative) including 
risk and threat considerations for each control area.  This effort, driven by the 
set of observations captured in our tool, will produce a high-level 
understanding of which business services may be most exposed and at risk. 
Incovate Solutions will then deliver a Security Program Assessment Report 
(a PowerPoint presentation) with an approximately 5-7 slide executive 
summary, coupled with a more detailed appendix (~10-12 slides based on 
the overall project).   The executive summary will cover what we did, what we 
found, how our observations impact Navajo County’s business and business

<!-- page 83 -->
Master Services Agreement and SOW #1  Navajo County 
 
Incovate Solutions LLC.   14 April 2025 
Sensitive and Confidential Information  Page 13 of 15 
strategy, and a prioritized roadmap of projects from Phase 4.   As noted 
below, Phase 4 output will be incorporated into the Phase 3 Deliverable.   
 
iv. Phase 4: Roadmap and Priority – In Phase 4, Incovate Solutions will 
identify an appropriate target state for Navajo County’s maturity against NIST 
CSF, with prioritized activities to close critical gaps based on the assessment 
results. Incovate Solutions will work closely with Navajo County to identify a 
strategic roadmap of recommended projects to improve security and begin 
building a sustainable information security program for Navajo County NIST 
CSF maturity.  Incovate Solutions will also consider and sequence each of 
the recommended initiatives (including “quick wins”), and work with Navajo 
County on high-level resource estimates for major recommendations. These 
recommendations make up the “next steps” for Navajo County to begin 
improving their security program based on strategic business goals, the 
observations from Phase 3, and the evolving threat environment.   
 
v. Briefings – Following completion of the assessment, Incovate Solutions will 
provide up to three (3) one-hour presentations of the final report to key 
leadership stakeholders using the executive deck, and will be available to 
discuss recommendations and the roadmap as outlined in the report. 
  
2. Potential Access to Personally Identifiable Information and/or Protected Health 
Information.  
  
As part of this Project, Incovate Solutions may be requested by Client to access certain 
personally identifiable information (PII) and/or Protected Health Information (PHI). If 
Incovate Solutions receives permission from Client to access such data, the parties shall 
follow the below procedures for the purposes of this SOW only:    
  
• Client will provide supervised access and applicable permission rights to 
Incovate Solutions for the sensitive data Client elects Incovate Solutions to view. 
Client will assign the appropriate Client points of contact who will collaborate with the 
Incovate Solutions team and confirm/approve the type of data Incovate Solutions 
may access.   
  
• Per mutual understanding, Incovate Solutions will only receive, store, or 
manipulate any data containing PII or PHI on or within the Client secure 
network/system. Incovate Solutions will access such data using Client-issued devices 
(i.e. Client issued laptops) connected to Client’s secured network/systems.  Client-
provided devices that will be utilized by Incovate Solutions will include the same level 
of  security standards as Client employees devices and shall be subject to the same 
controls, including any data use policies, authentication, or other means.  Incovate 
Solutions shall not  store, download,  or otherwise intentionally access any data 
containing PII or PHI on any  device that is not provided to it by Client.  
  
• In the event Client transmits PII or PHI inadvertently to Incovate Solutions, Client 
shall be solely liable for any resulting damages, and Incovate Solutions shall use 
commercially reasonable efforts to notify Client of such inadvertent disclosure and 
shall destroy or return such PII or PHI to Client upon discovering such accidental 
disclosure, provided that any failure to destroy or return accidentally disclosed PII or 
PHI shall not alter Client’s sole liability for any data disclosures or privacy breaches 
with respect to such information. .

Re:  Porter Mountain Road – Update topo / Right-of-way Survey for Porter Mountain Road 
WLB Project No.  119041A002   
Work Order:  WO-01 Porter Mountain Culvert Topograp hy and Right-of-Way Update. 
 
Dear Bryan, 
 
This letter presents our Work Order for updating th e topography along Porter Mountain Road at the culv ert 
crossing and preparing a Record of Survey for the Porter Mountain Road Right-of-way that can be the basis for 
preparation of easements on the United States Forest Service parcels for the construction of the new culverts. 
 
Project Understanding: Last summer the Town of Pinetop Lakeside installed a new asphalt pedestrian path 
along the west side of Porter Mountain Road, see Figure 1 below.  Since the path has modified the topography 
previously provided by the County for the culvert crossing WLB will perform a topographic survey to update 
the survey to show existing conditions. 
. 
 
Figure 1 – New Asphalt Path 
 
Since the roadway is within a an existing 100’ wide easement with United States Forest Services (USFS) WLB 
will also prepare a right-of-way survey to document the limits of the easement and prepare legal descriptions 
for any easements required for construction on USFS parcels.

<!-- page 95 -->
Bryan Cook, EIT  
Navajo County 
April 4, 2025 
Revised April 30, 2025 
Page 2 of 4 
 
120 N Beaver Street, Suite 100 • Flagstaff, Arizona 86001 • (928) 779-1500 • FAX (928) 779-1501  
 
Work Order WO-01 – SCOPE SERVICE: The scope of services for this task order includes the following tasks: 
 
Task 1 – Right-Of-Way Survey: 
WLB will prepare a Right-of-way Survey for the Porter Mountain right-of-way tying into the survey controlled 
used by Navajo County for the project topography.  WLB will record the survey with Navajo County to 
document the location of the easement across USFS land to reference for the preparation of easement 
descriptions needed for construction. 
 
Deliverable:  Record of Survey for the Porter Mountain Road right-of-way recorded with Navajo County 
 
Task 2 – Update Topographic Survey: 
Using the control provided by Navajo County for the topography for the culvert replacement WLB will survey 
the area of the crossing to Update the topography for use in preparing the construction documents.  The limit 
of the updated topography is an approximately 550’ long section to include the locations of the existing 
guardrail.  See Figure 2 below.  The updated topography survey will be merged into the base topography 
provided by Navajo County. 
 
 
Figure 2: Topographic survey update. 
 
Deliverable: Updated Topography for preparation of Construction Documents.  No official submittal will be 
made.

Task 3 – Legal Descriptions for Easements Outside of Right-Of-Way: 
WLB will prepare up to two legal descriptions for easements on USFS land outside of the existing 100’ wide 
easement for Porter Mountain Road Right-of-way.   The legal descriptions will include the description and an 
exhibit that will be used by Navajo County to obtain rights for the installation of the culvert outside if the 
existing right-of-way. 
 
Deliverable: Two Legal Descriptions and Exhibits for Easements outside of existing right-of-way. 
 
END WO-01 SCOPE OF SERVICES

Title: Arizona State Parks Project Sponsor Agreement
Submitted For: David Clouse, Sheriff 
Submitted By: Kimberley Willis, Sheriff's Office Finance Manager
Department: Sheriff 
Motion before the Board:
Approval of a project sponsor agreement from Arizona State Parks
Background:
A resolution was passed and adopted by the Navajo County Board of Supervisors on 4/22/25 for the
filing of an application for grant funding from the Arizona State Parks as a part of their OHV Law
Enforcement and Education Program. The Navajo County Sheriff's Office has been awarded funding for
the purchase of two (2) side-by-sides with LE lighting and communications, two (2) OHV tandem axle
trailers, and funding for OHV patrol hours. The total of the award is $275,000 with $211,000 designated
for the equipment and $64,000 for personnel costs. The Sheriff's Office will provide up to $25,000 as an
in-kind project match through staff time, which is part of the $275,000.

Motion before the Board:

and the Navajo County Adult Detention Center for Adult Detention Services.
Background:
An updated cooperative agreement between the BIA and the Nvajo County Adult Detention Center has
been generated for the housing of adult Tribal inmates who have been arrested for, charged with, or have
been convicted of Tribal violations occurring in Indian country. The current daily housing rate of $55 per
day, initiated in 2014, has been updated to reflect a current daily housing rate of $150 per Tribal inmate
per day. The term of this agreement is effective June 20, 2024, and expires on June 19, 2029.

Motion before the Board:
Approval of an IGA for the creation of and participation in the Arizona Child Abduction Response Team
(AZCART).
Background:
The Navajo County Sheriff's Office is a participating agency in the Arizona Child Abduction Response
Team and currently has a signed IGA which is in effect until June 30, 2030. However, the IGA has been
updated and all participating agencies are requested to sign the revised attached agreement. The updated
intergovernmental agreement holds the same term as the prior agreement, expiring June 30, 2030, with an
option for a 5-year extension upon request. The Arizona Child Abduction Response Team agency offers a
pool of specialized investigators, who are available to focus on dedicated and intensive investigative,
preventative, and general law enforcement efforts, primarily concerning cases involving abducted or
at-risk missing children.
