Project Overview
The Woodruff community in Navajo County (County) experiences frequent nuisance flooding, mostly along 
1st Street and County Road, due to the lack of drainage infrastructure. Currently, runoff flows along roadway 
alignments in undersized roadside ditches or adjacent properties, and ponds at intersections. Runoff 
generally flows south to north through the community. These conditions create impacts to resident vehicular 
ingress/egress and parking. The County would like to develop construction documents based on the 
drainage evaluation completed by Kimley-Horn in March 2025. This feasibility study developed drainage 
project alternatives with phasing and cost analyses. The County would like to develop construction 
documents for the selected alternative, and for both phases per the finalized evaluation memo dated 
3/12/25. 
Project Understanding and Approach
Plans, special provisions, and an engineer’s estimate of probably cost will be developed for the Phase 1 
and 2 improvements identified in the drainage evaluation. The final design effort will include field survey to 
support design, utility locating services, and utility coordination. Services not included in this scope of work 
include public involvement, geotechnical evaluations, and FEMA map revisions for work in the Little 
Colorado River floodplain.
Scope of Services
Kimley-Horn (KH) will provide the services specifically set forth below.
Task 1 Meetings and Coordination 
Kimley-Horn will participate in project meetings as follows:
•Project kickoff meeting and field review (at County Public Works and on site)
•Comment resolution meeting (teleconference)
•General project coordination as needed (teleconference)
KH will prepare and distribute meeting notes to the project team for the above meetings. KH will also 
conduct brief conference calls with the County Project Manager as needed.
KH will submit monthly invoices to the County. The monthly invoice will indicate the start date and end date 
of the invoice period. This task includes internal Kimley-Horn project coordination, team meetings and 
project administration.
The County will provide the following information to KH if available:
•Right-of-way and easement maps within the project area
•As-built construction plans as available for impacted streets and underground utilities
•Available utility information within the project vicinity
Task 2 Field Survey
KH will retain S&F Land Services as a subconsultant to conduct a field survey for this project. KH will review 
the field survey data provided by S&F Land Services and process the data for use in the design files. Field 
survey services per S&F Land Services’ scope of work are attached.

<!-- page 393 -->
Woodruff Drainage Improvements
Page 3 of 6
kimley-horn.com 1661 E Camelback Road, Suite 400, Phoenix, AZ 85016 602 944 5500
Task 3 Construction Documents
KH will prepare construction plans, an Engineer’s Cost Estimate (ECE), and Technical Special Provisions 
for the recommended drainage concept. KH will use the Navajo County Design & Construction Standards 
& Specifications (2025) as applicable. These standards and specifications will be supplemented by 
Maricopa Association of Governments (MAG) or Arizona Department of Transportation (ADOT) 
Specifications as needed. KH will prepare technical special provisions for construction work that would be 
required outside of the County, ADOT, and MAG standard specifications. Kimley-Horn will prepare 
submittals to the County for draft final and sealed final plans.
Kimley-Horn’s preliminary sheet count, based on 22-inch x 34-inch sheets, is as follows:
•Cover Sheet (1 Sheet)
•Legend, Notes and Quantity Summary (1 Sheet)
•Plan and Profile Sheets (12 Sheets, 1 inch = 20 feet)
•Drainage Detail Sheets (2 Sheets)
Task 3.1 – Draft Final Plans Submittal
The draft final submittal will consist of draft plans. KH will prepare an ECE and draft special provisions. 
Comments from this submittal will be reviewed and a response prepared. Changes will be incorporated into 
the sealed 100% plans as appropriate.
The draft final submittal will consist of the following:
•Full-size (22-in by 34-in) plans (pdf)
•Draft OPC with bid numbers matching the County’s standard bid item numbers
•Outline of technical special provisions
Task 3.2 – Final Sealed Plans Submittal
The final sealed submittal will consist of final sealed plans (pdf). A final ECE will be completed.
The final sealed plans submittal will consist of the following:
•Full-size (22-in by 34-in) sealed plans (pdf)
•“Red line” set of plans with written responses to the County’s comments from the previous submittal
•Final Technical Special provisions
•Final OPC with bid numbers matching the County’s standard bid item numbers
Task 4 Utility Locating Services
KH will initiate a Blue Stake request at the project onset and will collect any publicly available utility data 
from the County and private utility companies. 
KH will perform Level B designation prior to field survey. KH will coordinate with the field survey team to 
ensure all utilities designated are properly captured in the field. KH will use the field data to compile a utility 
base map as part of the project files.

<!-- page 394 -->
Woodruff Drainage Improvements
Page 4 of 6
kimley-horn.com 1661 E Camelback Road, Suite 400, Phoenix, AZ 85016 602 944 5500
Task 5 Utility Coordination
KH will contact private utility companies with services potentially impacted by the project for initial 
coordination. KH will identify potential utility conflicts during the development of the plans. KH will send out 
plans for conflict review at the draft final and sealed 100% stages to the utility companies and review and/or 
address any comments they may have. Kimley-Horn will coordinate with private utility companies during 
plan review. KH will coordinate with private utility companies to obtain final clearance letters prior to 
construction.
Task 6 Environmental Services
Section 404/401 Clean Water Act (CWA)
Aquatic Resource Delineation and Preliminary Jurisdictional Delineation Report
Based on a preliminary review of the site, jurisdictional wetlands and/or surface waters (aquatic features) 
have the potential to be present within the limits of the project site. Kimley-Horn will conduct one (1) day of 
field reconnaissance to delineate the extents of aquatic features within the project limits in accordance with 
the U.S. Army Corps of Engineers (Corps) 1987 Wetland Delineation Manual and applicable regional 
supplement. The results of the delineation will be summarized in a report, including a graphic depicting the 
limits of aquatic resources observed during the field delineation. One (1) upland and one (1) wetland Corps’ 
Wetland Determination form will be completed for each aquatic resource, if applicable. One (1) Ordinary 
High-Water Mark (OHWM) form will be completed for each surface water resource, if applicable. Aquatic 
resource locational data will be collected via handheld GNSS receiver.
The Report will consist of the following:
•Request for Corps Jurisdictional Determination Form (ENG Form 6247)
•Preliminary Jurisdictional Determination Form (ENG Form 6249)
•Site and landscape description
•Aquatic resource assessment
oVegetation
oSoils
oHydrology
oTable 1: Wetland Type and Size
oTable 2: Characteristics of Wetlands
oTable 3: Surface Water Type and Size
oTable 4: Characteristics of Surface Waters
•Figure 1. Vicinity Map
•Figure 2. USGS 7.5’ Quadrangle Map 
•Figure 3: Database Review Map (National Wetlands Inventory (NWI), United States Geological 
Survey (USGS) National Hydrography Dataset (NHD), and USGS Light Detection and Ranging 
(LiDAR))
•Figure 4: Aquatic Resource Delineation Map
•Figure 5: Natural Resources Conservation Services (NRCS) Hydric Soils Map
•Figure 6. FEMA Floodplain Map
•Ground Photographs
•KMZ of Potential WOTUS
Section 404 Nationwide Permit (NWP) Preconstruction Notification (PCN)
Kimley-Horn will prepare a PCN package including the following:

<!-- page 395 -->
Woodruff Drainage Improvements
Page 5 of 6
kimley-horn.com 1661 E Camelback Road, Suite 400, Phoenix, AZ 85016 602 944 5500
•PCN Form 
•Table 1: Impacts to Potential Waters of the U.S. 
•Figure 1. Impacts to Potential Waters of the U.S.  
•Biological Resources Memorandum (task below)
•Class I Literature Review (task below), Class III is excluded from services
•Design plans that include Impacts to Waters of the U.S. (60% Design)
•KMZ of Impact to WOTUS
Kimley-Horn will submit the PCN package to the Corps for review and one (1) round of comments.  
Biological Resources
Kimley-Horn will obtain and review an Official Species List for the project area from the U.S. Fish and 
Wildlife Service (USFWS) Information, Planning, and Consultation (IPaC) system to determine if 
threatened, endangered, proposed, and/or candidate species may occur in the project vicinity.  The Arizona 
Game and Fish Department (AGFD) on-line environmental review tool (OERT) report will be obtained to 
determine special status species are known to occur in the project vicinity.  Kimley-Horn will conduct one 
site visit to document site characteristics with ground photography. Results of this assessment will be 
included in a brief Biological Resources Memorandum. Kimley-Horn will submit the Biological Resources 
Memorandum to the Corps for review and one (1) round of comments.  
Cultural Resources
Because this project takes place on Navajo County ROW, the project requires compliance with the Arizona 
Antiquities Act (AAA) (ARS § 15-163 and § 41-841 et. Seq.) and ARS § 41-865. Because this project will 
require the Corps to issue a Section 404 permit, the project will also require compliance with Section 106 
of the National Historic Preservation Act of 1966, as amended. 
Kimley-Horn will conduct a Class I cultural resource records review. This review will identify the type, 
location, and distribution of previously recorded cultural resources and gather information on past cultural 
resource projects in the vicinity. Per State Historic Preservation Office (SHPO) requirements, the records 
review will include a 1-mile buffer around the project area. Kimley-Horn archaeologists will consult records 
from AZSITE, Arizona’s online cultural resources database, the Archaeological Records Office at the 
Arizona State Museum (ASM), records the Digital Archaeological Record (tDAR), General Land Office 
historic plats filed with the Bureau of Land Management, historic U.S. Geological Survey topographic maps, 
historic aerial photographs, the National Register of Historic Places, and the Arizona Register of Historic 
Places. 
The Class I report that Kimley-Horn will prepare will make recommendations regarding any additional 
cultural resource work that may need to be conducted for the project.
Task 7 Drainage Report
KH will compile a drainage report using calculations and analysis conducted as part of the evaluation 
memo. No new hydrology will be completed, but hydraulic calculations to size culverts and storm drain will 
be required. KH will use HY-8 and StormCAD software for these calculations. KH will also update 
hydraulic calculations for the open channel roadside ditches with the updated topographic data acquired 
as part of Task 2. All calculations with supporting exhibits and maps will be compiled into a final drainage 
report for review and approval by the County. This task assumes one round of review will be required.

Task 8 Bid Assistance
KH will provide up to twelve (12) hours of bid assistance and post design support as needed. It is assumed 
this effort will be to answer questions during the bidding process, and to respond to contractor requests for 
information during construction.
Services Not Included
Any other services, including but not limited to the following, are not included in this Agreement:  
•Potholing
•New hydrology calculations that were not part of the drainage evaluation previously completed
•Design of any roadway, traffic or lighting improvements
•Design of utility relocations
•Public meetings attendance
•Federal Emergency Management Agency (FEMA) map revisions
•Class III cultural resource survey and architectural analyses by an architectural historian
•Species specific surveys
•Construction administration
•As-built survey
•Any other services not specifically set forth in the above scope of services
Additional Services
Any services not specifically provided for in the above scope will be billed as additional services and 
performed at our then current hourly rates.

upon schedule.

RE: Woodruff AZ – Survey Proposal
Mr. Huber,
On behalf of S&F Land Services, I am pleased to submit this proposal to provide professional surveying
services for the Woodruff AZ project, as shown on the enclosed Exhibit A.
Scope of Work:
1. Topographic Survey:
a. See area of survey outlined in YELLOW on enclosed Exhibit A.
b. Limits of survey are:
i. Rights of Way within outlined area
ii. Little Colorado River crossing (Woodruff Rd)
1. 100 feet each direction.
2. Bed/bottom of river will be collected only as can be safely accessed.
3. NOTE INCLUDED: Private properties (blocks) within outlined area.
c. Full topographic survey for design:
i. Improvements
ii. All planimetric features
iii. Surface data to create contours at 1’ intervals.
d. Utilities:
i. Above ground evidence
ii. Underground utilities per Arizona811 (blue-stake) markings
1. S&F will submit requests 10 business days prior to fieldwork.
iii. Structures (manholes/catch basins): pipe inverts, size, direction, and material.
e. Trees: 6” DBH or greater
2. Monumentation:
a. Search for centerline, PLSS or other monumentation that may control the centerline of
subject rights-of-way.
b. NOTE: This does not include a complete right-of-way survey. Select monuments will be
searched for based of records available on-line, and recoverable from our topographic
survey mobilization.
3. Aerial Imagery:
a. As a part of our data collection workflow, the site will be flown with unpiloted aerial vehicle
to collect site imagery.
b. S&F will deliver an ortho-rectified aerial image of the site, georeferenced to overlay onto
the CAD Survey file.

Page 2 of 2
Assumptions:
i. NOT included in this scope of work (these can be added if required)
a. Centerline or Right-of-Way resolutions
b. Easement mapping
ii. Underground Utilities:
a. Public Utility Notification Center (one-call) underground utility markings are assumed to
have been completed prior to the date(s) of scheduled survey fieldwork - return trips related
to incomplete one-call marking at the time of field survey (if requested) will be billed on a
T&M basis.
b. S&F assumes no responsibility for the accuracy of the delineation of underground utilities
by utility locating firms and/or the respective utility owners, nor for the existence of any
buried objects. All utility locations should be field verified prior to construction.
iii. S&F will have unobstructed access to the subject property to complete the above scope of work.
iv. DATUM: Horizontal Datum will be State Plane Coordinates or as otherwise directed. Vertical Datum
will be per the jurisdiction in which the project is located or as otherwise directed.
Deliverables:
i. AutoCAD .dwg drawing file.
Schedule:
i. Deliverables could be furnished within approximately 4 weeks of contracted notice to proceed.
NOTE: A more aggressive schedule may be available
Fees:
The fees for the scope of services as described above will be on a fixed fee basis. Additional services
requested and approved by the Client not described herein will be billed as a Contract Addendum.
FEES $24,738
1. Topographic Survey
2. Monumentation
3. Aerial Imagery
EXPENSES invoiced at cost plus 10%
A. Plotting and Shipping (if hard copies requested)

proceed. Signing this document is a promise to pay for services as outlined above, and acknowledgment
of our enclosed terms and conditions. I appreciate the opportunity to provide this proposal for your
consideration.  If you have any questions or concerns, please feel free to call me at the phone number
listed above.
